## Finalized Report / Current Recommendation

The accepted ordinary-document callback reconstructs `NexusTK/util/PoolAllocator.h` and `NexusTK/util/PoolAllocator.cpp` as a standalone shared utility. The implemented unit consists of the 0x28-byte class, two private helper records, four public methods, two file-local chunk helpers, and one compiler cleanup funclet excluded from authored source. The existing nested emitter route is repaired without creating identities; all nine consumer includes, the missing EffectObjectPane pool declaration, all eight StringBase constructor counts, and the by-value `FreePoolChunkList` source shape now read back from generated output. Fifty-one changed by-* pages passed scoped validation and all eleven generated CPP/H artifacts passed physical and semantic reread. The bounded IDA recommendation remains one function comment, three address comments, and nineteen independently proved 40-byte pool materializations with one-channel regular comments. Fifteen destination names are newly created and must converge across item display plus both catalogs; four exact same-address catalog bindings are reasserted under split authority, where a blank `inspect_items.name` poststate is accepted only when the exact 40-byte typed item, both one-hit same-address catalogs, comment, bytes, xrefs, and neighbors all pass. All functions, frames, UDTs, wrappers, padding, bytes, and xrefs otherwise remain protected. Supervisor ownership covers Gate 2B runtime authority, backup, save, persisted readback, rollback safety, prerequisite handling, manual coverage, report execution, and archive handling; this report makes no assertion about current supervisor lifecycle state.

## Executive Recommendation

1. Keep every existing identity.
2. Make the class page the H owner and CPP prelude/outer emitter.
3. Nest Free under core so method order is constructor, Allocate, Free, destructor.
4. Keep both non-contiguous helpers file-owned after the methods.
5. Remove the layout empty emitter without duplicating the declaration.
6. Add the header to the allocator and nine consumers, emit the Effect pool, and add `64` to eight StringBase constructors.
7. Materialize each proven pool range with one exact public `make_data` request and add its exact regular address comment; for P03/P05/P08/P09 accept the proved split-authority blank item-display poststate only under the complete exact catalog/type/comment/bytes/xrefs/neighbor closure, require full item-display-plus-catalog convergence for the other fifteen names, and do not mutate any other entity.
8. End the report at the exact entity-level IDA actions and protections; the supervisor records the accepted Gate 2B authority, backup, save, persisted-readback, rollback-safety, and prerequisite audit in `tools/leaser/Agents/b-report-validation-audit.md` under UID0000MM before invoking the exact supervisor-only lifecycle command.

| Range, split, and padding | F01-F04 form the exact 0x4b13d0-0x4b1585 method island around E01; F05/F06 are exact non-contiguous helpers at 0x549bd0-0x549bf1 and 0x549c00-0x549c18. G01-G06 are exact CC padding/alignment, and all neighboring heads are protected. | No identity split or merge is justified. Preserve all existing identities, correct E01's endpoint to 0x4b151e, and emit helpers through routing rather than manufacturing a contiguous range. |
| Generated-output defects | Historical pre-callback snapshots had the class in CPP, no H/includes, wrong method order, partial Allocate EH behavior, an empty-emitter marker, absent Effect pool declaration, and eight one-argument StringBase pools. Callback validators and physical rereads show the complete H, exact nested CPP order, catch/unlock/rethrow, all includes/declarations, and two-argument string pools. | Ordinary generated-source defects are closed through formal channels; generated files remain read-only and any later supervisor authority uses a fresh physical reread rather than an old command receipt. |
| Source placement | Cross-subsystem reuse plus the existing valid `NexusTK/util/` root supports a standalone header/implementation. UID0000AM provides the H and CPP prelude; UID000152/000153 provide ordered methods; UID0001F4/00023L provide file-local helpers; consumer statics stay outside this compilation unit. | Final placement is `NexusTK/util/PoolAllocator.h` and `.cpp` with one declaration authority, one nested source route, no duplicate layout emission, and no additional report target. |
| Draft-C++ blockers | The earlier pointer-to-pointer F06 draft, missing Allocate exception release/rethrow, absent sibling H, incomplete include graph, Effect placeholder, and StringBase constructor arity were concrete blockers. Exact disassembly, layout, consumer, callback, validator, and generated receipts resolve each one. | Formal CPP/H in Section 22 is implementation-complete and C++03-compatible. Only irrecoverable original filename/helper/member spelling and compiler-format uncertainty remain as score caps, not code blockers. |

## Evidence Standards Used

- Half-open ranges use current function endpoints, instructions, and padding.
- Behavior uses current decompile plus disassembly where EH was omitted.
- Ownership uses direct callers, startup/cleanup wrappers, storage xrefs, and routes.
- Generated defects use explicitly dated physical CPP/H snapshots; generated authority at any applicable Gate 2A/execution boundary comes from a fresh supervisor physical reread.
- IDA recommendations separate direct facts, exact safe operations, protected no-change entities, and hard stops.
- Formal source uses human-written C++ without raw/compiler/decompiler names.
- Incoming xref totals use `xref_query.total`; direct callees use public `callees`; `func_profile` is authoritative here only for exact function bounds, sizes, current names, and prototypes.

## Positive Evidence Summary

- Six compact functions implement one fixed-block allocator with consistent this offsets.
- The 0x28 layout fits two integers, two list heads, and x86 CRITICAL_SECTION at +0x10.
- Chunk allocation is 4 + blocksPerChunk * blockSize; Allocate reverse-threads payload blocks.
- Nineteen startup and nineteen cleanup wrappers use the same class.
- Consumers span render, map, UI, and strings.
- String sizes equal a 12-byte reference-counted header plus payload and terminator.
- Current routing already supports NexusTK/util/PoolAllocator.cpp.
- Every pool has a distinct exact 40-byte zero-backed range, direct startup/use/free/shutdown xrefs, no field-aligned interior xref, and protected outer neighbors, so current public `make_data` can safely replace the fragmented one-byte item model without changing bytes or references.
- Fresh exact-name reads find no proposed-name catalog entry for fifteen pools and exactly one same-address entry in both independent catalogs for P03/P05/P08/P09; there is no different-address or duplicate-name collision.
- P03 working-session readback proves that same-address name authority can remain catalog-only after successful typed materialization: physical item/type/comment/bytes/xrefs/neighbor and both catalogs passed even though item display stayed blank.
- The strongest ownership chain is six reusable allocator bodies -> one 0x28 API/layout -> nineteen distributed consumers -> one existing util route. This is sufficient for a standalone utility even without an embedded filename literal.

Address regular/repeatable channels at 0x4b14a2, 0x4b14f0, and 0x4b1501 are absent before the proposed one-channel additions. The cleanup start has no incoming xref; the EH tail has one data xref from 0x65cb1c.

| UDT | Exact current declaration state | Source disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PoolAllocator` | size 40, UDT, one member `_opaque@0x0/40:unsigned __int8[40]` | Preserve IDA's conservative opaque carrier; formal H supplies proven source fields and size assertion. |
| `PoolChunkHeader` | size 4, UDT, `next@0x0/4:PoolChunkHeader *` | Preserve; private CPP record. |
| `PoolFreeBlock` | size 4, UDT, `next@0x0/4:PoolFreeBlock *` | Preserve; private CPP record. |

The cleanup range 0x4b14f0-0x4b151e has no function object and contains exactly fifteen instructions: push ESI/EDI, copy `this`, enter lock at `this+0x10`, pass `[this+8]` to 0x549c00, zero `[this+8]` and `[this+0xc]`, leave lock, restore, return. It is compiler-covered/no-code.

Pool-name authority is channel-qualified. In the fresh prestate transaction, `inspect_items` showed fifteen autogenerated `unk_*` names and empty item-display names for P03/P05/P08/P09. Anchored `entity_query(kind:names)` and exact-filter `list_globals` independently returned no proposed-name rows for the fifteen and exactly one identical same-address row for each of the four. The later unsaved P03 application proved that the catalog/item-display split can persist after physical materialization: `make_data` reported `g_soundObjectPanePool`, the physical item became one exact 40-byte `PoolAllocator`, and both catalogs retained one exact same-address row, while `inspect_items.name` remained empty. Therefore item display remains mandatory name authority for the fifteen new-name rows, but the two catalogs jointly remain name authority for the four pre-existing same-address rows when every physical/protected-state check passes. No failed-transaction in-memory state is canonical authority.

## Documentation Evidence And IDA Status

Gate 1 pre-callback documentation state: the class page has source material in CPP while H is blank. Layout has an active emitter with blank channels. Core's broad fragment forces destructor before separately emitted Free. Target documentation uses stale cleanup endpoint 0x004b151d and omits the EH tail. The dated command-26323, supervisor command-26351, and later B001 command-26355 physical snapshots independently show the same routed state: all six authored bodies and the by-value Free helper survive, while declaration placement, includes, method order, Allocate EH behavior, sibling H, and empty-marker output remain defective. Those receipts establish dated evidence only; generated authority at any applicable Gate 2A/execution boundary requires the supervisor's fresh CPP/H reread. Dated item-display inspection found fifteen autogenerated `unk_*` names and empty displays for Sound, HitBar, Item, and Flying; the fresh failed Gate 2B transaction now proves that those four empty displays coexist with exact same-address bindings in both public name catalogs. Every base is still a one-byte undefined item with an attached `PoolAllocator` or `struct PoolAllocator` display spelling, and all nineteen regular/repeatable comment channels are blank.
Callback documentation state: UID0000AM now owns the exact CPP prelude and complete H; UID000152/000153/0001F4/00023L form the required nested source order; UID0001VK is non-emitting; all consumer emitter channels and pool declarations read back exactly. The fragmented pool IDA prestates are the documented B001 callback observation; their materialization and verification are supervisor-owned.

The materialization question is resolved rather than deferred. For each pool, `inspect_items` proves separate one-byte undefined items across the exact 40-byte range, `get_bytes` proves forty zero bytes, all field-aligned interior xref queries are zero, base xrefs account for startup/use/free/shutdown, and predecessor/successor queries prove non-overlap. Public `make_data` supports exact type, name, and `delete_existing:true`, recreates the item, then reports the requested name. Therefore the safest bounded improvement is one exact 40-byte `PoolAllocator` data item per base followed by one regular address comment: fifteen names are newly assigned only while both catalogs are empty and must appear in item display plus both catalogs afterward; the four exact same-address catalog bindings are reasserted only while both catalogs return exactly one identical target row, and their item display may remain empty after materialization under complete split-authority closure. Any changed byte, boundary, interior xref, different-address/duplicate name, catalog split outside the four recorded rows, type-size mismatch, missing comment, nonblank repeatable channel, or protected-neighbor mismatch stops before that row.

No by-item owner matches. Relevant types are PoolAllocatorLayout and RefCountedStringBufferHeader. The latter is 12 bytes and explains string blocks: ANSI N = 12 + (N+1), wide N = 12 + 2(N+1). PoolAllocator static-instance and StringBase globals are inventory owners, not allocator source owners.

## Ranked Ownership Analysis

1. PoolAllocator standalone utility: accepted.
2. Consumer files: accepted for nineteen static objects and generated wrappers.
3. StringBase: accepted only for eight string pools/use sites.
4. Object-pane, Motion, UserPane: accepted only for their pools/use sites.
5. StringUtil and ThreadSafeNodeList: rejected as implementation owners.

## Source Placement

Class emits complete H and CPP prelude plus outer CHILDREN. Core emits constructor, Allocate, nested CHILDREN, destructor. Free becomes core child position 10. Allocate/free helpers remain file-owned class children positions 20/30. Layout remains class-owned but contributes no duplicate declaration; its marker says the class H emits layout.

CPP order: include, records/prototypes, constructor, Allocate, Free, destructor, allocate helper, free helper. H order: guard, Windows dependency, record forwards, class, x86 assertion.

Includes: "PoolAllocator.h" in StringBase.cpp; "../util/PoolAllocator.h" in LivingObjectPane, StaticObjectPane, SoundObjectPane, ItemObjectPane, AttachedObjectPane, Motion, Effects; "../../util/PoolAllocator.h" in ui/panels/UserPane.cpp.

## Range / Split / Padding / Reclassification Analysis

No split is justified. The cleanup endpoint is half-open 0x004b151e: retn occupies bytes through 0x004b151d and 0x004b151e-0x004b1520 is padding. It has no direct start xref/current function, but lock/list-clear/unlock behavior and EH placement establish compiler output.

0x004b14a2-0x004b14b8 is Allocate, not padding: it leaves the critical section and calls _CxxThrowException(0,0). The current incoming data xref `0x0065cb1c -> 0x004b14a2` confirms the EH landing path. All six padding gaps are non-entities as inventoried.

## Negative Evidence Summary

- No filename, RTTI, or debug literal names the source file.
- No normal caller/pointer targets 0x004b14f0.
- No evidence supports ThreadSafeNodeList.cpp.
- No evidence supports string/object private allocator ownership.
- No evidence supports a by-item page or new split.
- No evidence supports changing six prototypes, helper UDTs, or frames.
- No pool row permits materialization unless its exact 40-byte zero range, fragmented one-byte items, base/interior xrefs, type size, three-channel name prestate, and outer neighbors all match: both catalogs empty for fifteen rows, or one identical same-address catalog hit in both channels for P03/P05/P08/P09.
- No evidence supports requiring `inspect_items.name` to become nonempty after a same-address reassertion: P03 disproved that prior postcondition while preserving exact physical item, type, catalogs, comment, bytes, xrefs, and neighbor state. This negative does not weaken the fifteen newly created-name rows.
- CRITICAL_SECTION is platform ABI; do not invent a lock wrapper.

## IDA Rename / Type / Comment Recommendations

All requests below are proposals for a later supervisor-owned isolated transaction. `DB` means only the database handle returned by that transaction's `idb_open`; no dated read-only or failed Gate 2B transaction is ever reused. Regular and repeatable channels are distinct. The persistent mutation topology remains exactly 42: 19 `make_data`, 1 `set_function_comments`, and 22 `set_address_comments` (3 compiler/EH plus 19 pool comments). All nineteen `make_data` requests retain their embedded exact names; fifteen create previously unused names and require item-display/catalog convergence, while P03/P05/P08/P09 reassert exact same-address catalog bindings under the split-authority postcondition. There are no separate `rename`, `set_type`, UDT, frame, wrapper, byte, or function-boundary mutations.

### Supervisor-Owned Gate 2B Boundary

This report contains only the exact structured entity actions and protected readbacks through F02, E01, and P01-P19. The removed legacy operational package is preserved verbatim only in the [inert removed-material companion](0000MM-PoolAllocator-file-source-quality-removed.md); that companion is non-authoritative and must not be executed or submitted to `execute_report`.

At Gate 2B, the supervisor must record accepted fresh authority, backup safety, save and persisted readback, and rollback/restoration safety around the entity actions in `tools/leaser/Agents/b-report-validation-audit.md` under UID0000MM before invoking the exact supervisor-only lifecycle command. Any unavailable prerequisite, authority mismatch, backup defect, save/readback failure, or rollback-safety defect fails closed before report execution. This report does not prescribe a transaction program, command sequence, process launcher, or filesystem operation package.

```cpp
#include "PoolAllocator.h"

#include <new>

struct PoolChunkHeader
{
    PoolChunkHeader *next;
};

struct PoolFreeBlock
{
    PoolFreeBlock *next;
};

static PoolChunkHeader *AllocatePoolChunk(
    PoolChunkHeader **chunkListHead,
    int blocksPerChunk,
    int blockSize);
static void FreePoolChunkList(PoolChunkHeader *chunkList);

PoolAllocator::PoolAllocator(int blockSize, int blocksPerChunk)
    : m_blockSize(blockSize),
      m_blocksPerChunk(blocksPerChunk),
      m_chunkList(0),
      m_freeList(0)
{
    InitializeCriticalSection(&m_lock);
}

void *PoolAllocator::Allocate()
{
    EnterCriticalSection(&m_lock);

    try
    {
        if (m_freeList == 0)
        {
            PoolChunkHeader *chunk = AllocatePoolChunk(
                &m_chunkList,
                m_blocksPerChunk,
                m_blockSize);
            char *block = reinterpret_cast<char *>(chunk + 1);

            block += (m_blocksPerChunk - 1) * m_blockSize;
            for (int index = m_blocksPerChunk - 1; index >= 0; --index)
            {
                PoolFreeBlock *freeBlock =
                    reinterpret_cast<PoolFreeBlock *>(block);
                freeBlock->next = m_freeList;
                m_freeList = freeBlock;
                block -= m_blockSize;
            }
        }

        PoolFreeBlock *block = m_freeList;
        m_freeList = block->next;
        LeaveCriticalSection(&m_lock);
        return block;
    }
    catch (...)
    {
        LeaveCriticalSection(&m_lock);
        throw;
    }
}

void PoolAllocator::Free(void *block)
{
    if (block == 0)
        return;

    EnterCriticalSection(&m_lock);
    PoolFreeBlock *freeBlock = static_cast<PoolFreeBlock *>(block);
    freeBlock->next = m_freeList;
    m_freeList = freeBlock;
    LeaveCriticalSection(&m_lock);
}

PoolAllocator::~PoolAllocator()
{
    EnterCriticalSection(&m_lock);
    FreePoolChunkList(m_chunkList);
    m_chunkList = 0;
    m_freeList = 0;
    LeaveCriticalSection(&m_lock);
    DeleteCriticalSection(&m_lock);
}

static PoolChunkHeader *AllocatePoolChunk(
    PoolChunkHeader **chunkListHead,
    int blocksPerChunk,
    int blockSize)
{
    PoolChunkHeader *chunk = static_cast<PoolChunkHeader *>(
        ::operator new(sizeof(PoolChunkHeader) + blocksPerChunk * blockSize));
    chunk->next = *chunkListHead;
    *chunkListHead = chunk;
    return chunk;
}

static void FreePoolChunkList(PoolChunkHeader *chunkList)
{
    while (chunkList != 0)
    {
        PoolChunkHeader *chunk = chunkList;
        chunkList = chunk->next;
        ::operator delete(chunk);
    }
}
```

```h
#ifndef NEXUSTK_UTIL_POOLALLOCATOR_H
#define NEXUSTK_UTIL_POOLALLOCATOR_H

#include <windows.h>

struct PoolChunkHeader;
struct PoolFreeBlock;

class PoolAllocator
{
public:
    PoolAllocator(int blockSize, int blocksPerChunk);
    ~PoolAllocator();

    void *Allocate();
    void Free(void *block);

private:
    int m_blockSize;
    int m_blocksPerChunk;
    PoolChunkHeader *m_chunkList;
    PoolFreeBlock *m_freeList;
    CRITICAL_SECTION m_lock;
};

typedef char PoolAllocatorSizeMustBe40[
    sizeof(PoolAllocator) == 0x28 ? 1 : -1];

#endif

Helper calls express observed new/free semantics without raw library labels.
